Curved twisting morphisms and Bar-Cobar adjunctions at the operadic level

The curved Koszul duality established in the previous section at the groupoid-colored level gives a
curved twisting morphism

\kappa
between the groupoid-colored curved cooperad encoding curved partial (co)operads
c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
and the groupoid-colored operad encoding unital partial (co)operads
u\mathcal{O}
. This curved twisting morphism induces two different Bar-Cobar adjunctions.

The first Bar-Cobar adjunction induced by
\kappa
is between dg unital partial operads and conilpotent curved partial cooperads. It will be shown to be isomorphic to the Bar-Cobar adjunction defined in
[
Gri19
, Section 4.1]
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
B
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

On the other hand, using the generalization of
[
GL18
]
to the groupoid-colored setting, we obtain a new complete Bar-Cobar adjunction:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\text{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between complete curved
absolute
partial operads and counital partial cooperads. Constructing this second adjunction is the main goal of this section.

6.1.
Curved twisting morphisms and curved pre-Lie algebras

In order to defined curved twisting morphisms in the first place, we introduce a new type of structure, called
curved pre-Lie algebras
. In this type of algebras, the curvature has to satisfy a special condition, called the
left-nucleus condition
. This condition comes from the deformation theory. More precisely, it appears in
[
DSV18
, Proposition 1.1, Chapter 4]
for the following reason: one can show that a dg pre-Lie algebra is
twistable
by a Maurer-Cartan element if and only if this element satisfies the extra condition of being left-nucleus.

Definition 6.1

(Curved pre-Lie algebra)
.

A
curved pre-Lie algebra

(\mathfrak{g},\{-,-\},d_{\mathfrak{g}},\vartheta)
amounts to the data of a pre-Lie algebra
(\mathfrak{g},\{-,-\})
, a derivation
d_{\mathfrak{g}}
with respect to
\{-,-\}
of degree
-1
, and a morphism of pdg modules of degree
-2

\vartheta:\mathbb{K}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}
. The data of this morphism is equivalent to the data of an element
\vartheta(1)\coloneqq\vartheta
in
\mathfrak{g}_{-2}
. They are subject to the following conditions.

(1)

The element
\vartheta
has to be
left-nucleus
, that is, for all
\mu,\nu
in
\mathfrak{g}
:

\{\vartheta,\{\mu,\nu\}\}=\{\{\vartheta,\mu\},\nu\}~.

(2)

Moreover, for all
\mu
in
\mathfrak{g}
:

d_{\mathfrak{g}}(\mu)=\{\vartheta,\mu\}-\{\mu,\vartheta\}~.

(3)

And finally,
d_{\mathfrak{g}}(\vartheta)=0
.

In the same spirit as for curved Lie algebras and curved associative algebras, one can define a curved partial operad,
cp\mathcal{L}ie
, that encodes curved pre-Lie algebras. Let
H
be the pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module
(\mathbb{K}.\vartheta,0,\mathbb{K}[\mathbb{S}_{2}].\nu,0,\cdots)
endowed with the zero pre-differential.

Definition 6.2

(Curved operad encoding curved pre-Lie algebras)
.

The curved partial operad
cp\mathcal{L}ie
is given by the presentation:

cp\mathcal{L}ie\coloneqq\overline{\mathscr{T}}(H)/(D)~,

where
(D)
is the operadic ideal generated by the following relations:

(1)

The right pre-Lie relation, already present in the classical pre-Lie operad, given by:

\includegraphics[width,scale]{prelie.eps}~.

(2)

The left-nucleus relation stating for the curvature
\vartheta
, given by:

\includegraphics[width,scale]{nucleus.eps}~.

It is endowed with the curvature
\Theta_{cp\mathcal{L}ie}(\mathrm{id})\coloneqq\nu\circ_{1}\vartheta-\nu\circ_{2}\vartheta~.

Proposition 6.3
.

The data
(cp\mathcal{L}ie,0,\Theta_{cp\mathcal{L}ie})
forms a curved partial operad. The category of curved
cp\mathcal{L}ie
-algebras is equivalent to the category of curved pre-Lie algebras.

Furthermore, the morphism of curved partial operads
c\mathcal{L}ie\longrightarrow c\mathcal{A}ss
given by the skew-symmetrization of the Lie bracket factors through the curved partial operad
cp\mathcal{L}ie
.

Proof.

Let us show that the data forms a curved partial operad. The proof is quite similar to that of Lemma
2.17
. In order to check that
[\nu\circ_{1}\vartheta-\nu\circ_{2}\vartheta,-]=0
, since it is a derivation with respect partial compositions, it is enough to test this equality on the generators. First,
[\nu\circ_{1}\vartheta-\nu\circ_{2}\vartheta,\vartheta]=0
is evident. Secondly, expanding
[\nu\circ_{1}\vartheta-\nu\circ_{2}\vartheta,\nu]
makes six terms that appear: it is straightforward to check that four of them cancel because of the right pre-Lie relation and the last two because of the left-nucleus relation. Proving that this curved partial operad encodes curved pre-Lie algebras is completely analogous to Lemma
2.17
. One checks that the map
c\mathcal{L}ie\longrightarrow cp\mathcal{L}ie
given on generators by
\beta\mapsto\nu-\nu^{(12)}
and
\zeta\mapsto\vartheta
is indeed a morphism of curved partial operads. Furthermore, there is a morphism of curved partial operads
cp\mathcal{L}ie\longrightarrow c\mathcal{A}ss
simply given by
\nu\mapsto\mu
and
\vartheta\mapsto\phi
. Their composition gives back the morphism constructed in Lemma
2.22
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Contrary to the case of Lie algebras and associative algebras, we had to introduce a new relation into the curved partial operad that encodes curved pre-Lie algebras. In the previous cases, this meant that the curved homotopy version of them was given simply by adding a curvature to the classical homotopy version. For instance, a curved
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra is just an
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra with an added structure of a curvature; the relations of a curved
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra are clearly analogous to those of an
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra. In the curved pre-Lie case, more structure will appear when one resolves the left-nucleus relation up to homotopy. Nevertheless, since the left-nucleus relation is not even quadratic, one would first need an expanded Koszul duality in order to treat this case.

We leave it to the reader to generalize the definition of curved partial operads to the
\mathbb{S}
-colored setting. (See Definition
5.50
for a similar definition).

Lemma 6.5

(Totalization of a curved partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad)
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\{\circ_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{G}},\Theta_{\mathcal{G}})
be a curved partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad. The totalization of
\mathcal{G}
given by

\prod_{(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r};n)\in\mathbb{N}^{r+1}}\left(\mathcal{G}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r};n)^{\left(\mathbb{S}_{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{n_{r}}\right)~\wr~\mathbb{S}_{r}}\right)^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}

forms a curved pre-Lie algebra, where the bracket is defined like in Proposition
5.26
and where the curvature is given by

\vartheta(1)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\theta_{n}~.

Proof.

Let
g
be in
\mathcal{G}(n_{1},\cdot,n_{r};n)
, we have that
d_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(g)=\theta_{n}\circ_{1}g-\sum_{i=0}^{r}g\circ_{i}\theta_{n_{i}}
since
\mathcal{G}
is a curved partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad. The bracket
\vartheta(1)\star g=\theta_{n}\circ_{1}g
since only
\theta_{n}
matches the appropriate color. Similarly,
g\star\vartheta(1)=\sum_{i=0}^{r}g\circ_{i}\theta_{n_{i}}
. Since each
\theta_{n}
is an arity one operation, by the sequential and parallel axioms of a partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad, the curvature satisfies the
left-nucleus
relation of a curved pre-Lie algebra.
∎

Lemma 6.6

(Curved convolution partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad)
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{G}})
be a dg unital partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad and let
(\mathcal{V},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{V}},\Theta_{\mathcal{V}})
be a curved partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored cooperad. The convolution partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad
\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{G})
forms a curved partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad endowed with the curvature:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}:\mathcal{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathcal{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

Proof.

Let
\alpha
be in
\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{G})(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r};n)
, we have that:

\partial^{2}(\alpha)=-(-1)^{2.|\alpha|}\alpha\circ d_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}=\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}\circ_{1}\alpha-\sum_{i=0}^{r}\alpha\circ_{i}\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}~,

since
d_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}=0
and
d_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}=(\mathrm{id}\circ_{(1)}\Theta_{\mathcal{V}}-\Theta_{\mathcal{V}}\circ\mathrm{id})\cdot\Delta_{(1)}~.

∎

Definition 6.7

(Maurer-Cartan of a curved pre-Lie)
.

Let
(\mathfrak{g},\{-,-\},d_{\mathfrak{g}},\vartheta)
be a curved pre-Lie algebra. A
Maurer-Cartan element

\alpha
is a degree
-1
element that of
\mathfrak{g}
that satisfies the following equation:

d_{\mathfrak{g}}(\alpha)+\{\alpha,\alpha\}=\vartheta~.

Remark 6.8
.

The set of Maurer-Cartan elements of a curved pre-Lie (or similarly a curved Lie) algebra can be empty. A curvature term
\vartheta\neq 0
stops
0
in
\mathfrak{g}_{-1}
from being a canonical Maurer-Cartan element in
\mathfrak{g}
.

Definition 6.9

(Curved
\mathbb{S}
-colored twisting morphism)
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{G}})
be a unital partial dg
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad and let
(\mathcal{V},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{V}},\Theta)
be a curved partial
\mathcal{S}
-colored cooperad. A
curved twisting morphism

\alpha
between
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{G}
is a morphism of pdg
\mathbb{S}
-color schemes
\alpha:\mathcal{V}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}
of degree
-1
that satisfies the Maurer-Cartan equation in the totalization of the curved convolution
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad
\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{G})
. Otherwise stated,
\alpha
satisfies:

\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}~.

Lemma 6.10
.

The
\mathbb{S}
-color scheme morphism
\kappa:\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O})^{\vee}\longrightarrow u\mathcal{O}
given by

\kappa:\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\twoheadrightarrow sE\oplus sJ\cong E\oplus U\hookrightarrow u\mathcal{O}

is a curved twisting morphism. Here
\mathcal{S}
denotes the operadic suspension of
c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
.

Proof.

Notice that
\partial(\kappa)=0
since the pre-differentials are null. Let us show that

\kappa\star\kappa=\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}~.

The morphism of
\mathbb{S}
-color schemes
\kappa\star\kappa
is non-zero only on elements of weight two in
c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
. These are elements which can informally be written as
\gamma_{i}^{n+m-1,k}\circ_{1}\gamma_{j}^{n,m}
and
\gamma_{i}^{n,k+m-1}\circ_{2}\gamma_{j}^{k,m}
, or as
\gamma_{i}^{1,n}\circ_{1}\theta
and
\gamma_{i}^{n,1}\circ_{2}\theta
. One computes that
\kappa\star\kappa
of the first kind of weight two elements is zero because of the Koszul signs, by using the sequential and parallel relations. On the second kind of weight two elements,
\kappa\star\kappa
is equal to
\gamma_{i}^{1,n}\circ_{1}u
and
\gamma_{i}^{n,1}\circ_{2}u
. Using the unital relation in
u\mathcal{O}
, these are both equal to
\mathrm{id}_{n}
, the operadic unit of the
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad
u\mathcal{O}
. Hence
\kappa\star\kappa
is equal to
\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}
.
∎

Notation
.

Let
f:M\longrightarrow N
be a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules of degree
0
and
g:M\longrightarrow N
be a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules degree
p
. We denote
\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r})(f,g)
the map

\sum_{i=1}^{r}f(n_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes f(n_{i-1})\otimes g(n_{i})\otimes f(n_{i+1})\otimes\cdots\otimes f(n_{r}):M(n_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes M(n_{r})\longrightarrow N(n_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes N(n_{r})

which is an
\mathbb{S}_{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{n_{r}}\wr\mathbb{S}_{r}
-equivariant morphism of degree
p
. Let
E
be a graded
\mathbb{S}
-color scheme. The family of maps
\{\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r})(f,g)\}
induces a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules of degree
p
:

\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(E)(M)\longrightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(E)(N)

by applying
\mathrm{id}_{E}\otimes\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r})(f,g)
to each component. By a slight abuse of notation, this morphism will be denoted by
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id}_{E})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(f,g))~.
Likewise, it induces a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules of degree
p
:

\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(E)(M)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(E)(N)

by applying
\mathrm{Hom}(id_{E},\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(n_{1},\cdots,n_{r})(f,g))
to each component. By a slight abuse of notation, this morphism will be denoted by
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id}_{E})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(f,g))~.

6.2.
Classical Bar-Cobar adjunction relative to
\kappa

The first adjunction induced by
\kappa
will be an adjunction between dg
u\mathcal{O}
-algebras and curved
\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})^{u}
-coalgebras. That is, between dg unital partial operads and shifted conilpotent curved partial cooperads.

Definition 6.11

(Bar-Cobar constructions relative to
\kappa
)
.

Using
\kappa
, one can define two functor:

(1)

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}})
a dg
u\mathcal{O}
-algebra. Its
Bar construction relative to

\kappa
, denoted by
\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}
, is given by the cofree
\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})^{u}
-coalgebra
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})^{u})(\mathcal{P})
. Its pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{bar}}
is given by the sum of two terms
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The first term is given by

d_{1}\coloneqq\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id},d_{\mathcal{P}}))~.

The second term
d_{2}
is the unique coderivation extending:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}

(2)

Let
(\mathcal{C},\Delta_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a curved
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-coalgebra. Its
Cobar construction relative to

\kappa
, denoted by
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathcal{C}
, is given by the free
u\mathcal{O}
-algebra
\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{C})
. Its differential
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}
is the sum of two terms
d_{1}
and
d_{1}
. The first term is given by

d_{1}\coloneqq-\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id},d_{\mathcal{C}}))~.

The second term
d_{2}
is the unique derivation extending:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id})}

Lemma 6.12
.

There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})^{u}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~u\mathcal{O}\text{-}\mathsf{alg}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{B}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

The proof is a minor generalization of a the adjunction induced by a curved twisting morphism at the level of algebras in
[
HM12
, Section 5]
.
∎

This adjunction relative to
\kappa
is in fact isomorphic to the adjunction between conilpotent curved coaugmented cooperads and operads introduced in
[
Gri19
, Section 4.1]
. Before proving this result, we briefly recall the definition of this adjunction.

Definition 6.13

(
[
Gri19
, Definition 58]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad. The
Bar construction

\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
of
\mathcal{P}
is given by:

\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\coloneqq\left(\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{c}(s\mathcal{P}\oplus\nu),d_{\mathrm{bar}}=d_{1}+d_{2},\Theta_{\mathrm{bar}}\right)~,

where
\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{c}(s\mathcal{P}\oplus\nu)
is the cofree conilpotent partial pdg cooperad generated by the dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
s\mathcal{P}\oplus\vartheta
. Here
\nu
is an arity
1
and degree
-2
generator. It is endowed the pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{bar}}
, given by the sum of
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is the unique coderivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{c}(s\mathcal{P}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sd_{\mathcal{P}}}

The term
d_{2}
comes from the
structure
of a unital partial operad on
\mathcal{P}
, it is given by the unique coderivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{c}(s\mathcal{P}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I.\nu\oplus(s\mathcal{P}\circ_{(1)}s\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\eta+s^{2}\gamma_{(1)}}

It is also endowed with the following curvature:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathrm{bar}}:\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{c}(s\mathcal{P}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I.\nu}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}}

The resulting Bar construction of
\mathcal{P}
forms a conilpotent curved partial cooperad.

Definition 6.14

(
[
Gri19
, Definition 60]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Theta_{\mathcal{C}})
be a curved partial cooperad. The
Cobar construction

\Omega\mathcal{C}
of
\mathcal{C}
is given by:

\Omega\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\left(\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}),d_{\mathrm{cobar}}=d_{1}-d_{2}\right)~,

where
\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C})
is the free unital partial operad generated by the pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}
. It is endowed with the differential
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}
given by the difference of
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is the unique derivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}d_{\mathcal{C}}}

The term
d_{2}
comes from the
structure
of a conilpotent curved partial cooperad on
\mathcal{C}
, it is given by the unique derivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\circ_{(1)}s^{-1}\mathcal{C})\oplus\I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}\Delta_{(1)}+s^{-1}\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}}

The resulting Cobar construction of
\mathcal{C}
forms a dg unital partial operad.

Remark 6.15
.

The Cobar constructions
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is not augmented because the canonical morphism
\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C})\twoheadrightarrow\I
does not commute with the differentials in general. Indeed,
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}(\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{id}))
is the trivial tree
|
. It is therefore augmented if and only if the curvature
\Theta_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{id})
is zero.

The Bar-Cobar constructions described above also form an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proposition 6.16

(Mise en abîme)
.

The Bar-Cobar adjunction relative to
\kappa
given by
\Omega_{\kappa}\dashv\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}
is naturally isomorphic to the Bar-Cobar adjunction
\Omega\dashv\mathrm{B}
constructed in
[
Gri19
]
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad. One uses the bijection given in the proof of Proposition
5.58
to construct a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}~.

∎

Corollary 6.17
.

This gives another proof that the Bar-Cobar adjunction
\Omega\dashv\mathrm{B}
constructed in
[
Gri19
]
is a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

Since we consider a transferred structure on curved conilpotent partial cooperads, proving that this adjunction is a Quillen equivalence simply amounts to show that for any unital partial operad
\mathcal{P}
, the unit

\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}~,

is a quasi-isomorphism. This follows from the fact that the Koszul complex of the curved twisting morphism
\kappa:\mathcal{S}\otimes(c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\longrightarrow u\mathcal{O}
that induces this Bar-Cobar adjunction is acyclic, using analogous arguments to
[
Val20
, Theorem 2.6]
.
∎

The notion of curved twisting morphism between conilpotent curved partial cooperad and dg unital partial operad is again encoded by a curved pre-Lie algebra.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be a curved partial operad. The totalization of
\mathcal{P}
given by

\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{P}(n)^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}~,

together with its pre-Lie bracket and endowed with the curvature
\vartheta(1)\coloneqq\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathrm{id})
forms a curved pre-Lie algebra.

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to Lemma
6.5
.
∎

Lemma 6.19

(Curved convolution operad)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Theta)
be a curved partial cooperad. The convolution partial pdg operad
(\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\{\circ_{i}\},\partial)
forms a curved partial operad endowed with the curvature given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}(\mathrm{id}):\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to Lemma
6.6
.
∎

Definition 6.20

(Curved twisting morphism)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Theta)
be a curved partial cooperad. A
curved twisting morphism

\alpha
is a Maurer-Cartan element curved pre-Lie algebra given by the curved convolution operad:

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C}(n),\mathcal{P}(n))~.

This is the data of a morphism of
\mathbb{S}
-modules
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
of a degree
-1
such that:

\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}(\mathrm{id})~.

The set of twisting morphism between
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
will be denoted
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
.

The set of curved twisting morphism between conilpotent curved partial cooperads and unital partial dg operads defines a bifunctor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(-,-):\left(\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}\right)^{\text{op}}\times\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}~,}

which is represented on both sides by the Bar-Cobar construction defined before.

Proposition 6.21

(
[
Gri19
, Proposition 63]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Theta)
be a conilpotent curved partial cooperad. There are isomorphism:

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}}(\Omega\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}}(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})~,

which are natural in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
.

6.3.
Complete Bar-Cobar adjunction relative to
\kappa

We introduce a new adjunction, which we call the
complete Bar-Cobar adjunction
, between complete curved absolute partial operads and dg counital partial cooperads. This adjunction is again induced by the curved twisting morphism
\kappa
, using the techniques developed in
[
GL18
]
. In order to construct it, we need a mild generalization of the results in
[
Ane14
]
, in which the author only considers set-colored operads.

Theorem 6.22
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\gamma,\eta,d_{\mathcal{G}})
be a dg
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad. The category of dg
\mathcal{G}
-coalgebras is comonadic, that is, there exists a comonad
(\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G}),\omega,\xi)
in the category of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules such that the category of dg
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})
-coalgebras is equivalent to the category of dg
\mathcal{G}
-coalgebras.

The endofunctor
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})
is given by the following pullback:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G}\circ\mathcal{G})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\gamma)}

in the category
\mathsf{End}(\mathsf{dg}~\mathbb{S}\textsf{-}\mathsf{mod})
. It gives a pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})(M)\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G}\circ\mathcal{G})(M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}(M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}}(M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\gamma)(M)}

for any dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
M
. Here
p_{1}
is a monomorphism since
\varphi_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{G}}(M)
is also a monomorphism. The structural map of the comonad
\omega(M):\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})(M)\longrightarrow\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})\circ\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})(M)
is given by the map
p_{2}
in the previous pullback, as its image lies in
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})\circ\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})(M)
instead of
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})(M)
. The counit is given of the comonad
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi(M):\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{G})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}(M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\eta)(id_{M})}

Proof.

In order to prove the first result, one has to generalize the framework of
[
Ane14
]
from set-colored operads to groupoid-colored operads. This is purely formal: the underlying theory upon which the result is obtained is the formalism of analytic functors and operads developed in
[
GJ17
]
. In
loc.cit
, the formalism is already developed in the case where the colors form a category.
Mutatis mutandis
, one can perform the same proofs using this general formalism. Thus the category of dg
\mathcal{G}
-coalgebras is indeed comonadic, by
[
Ane14
, Theorem 2.7.11]
.

The second statement is equivalent to claiming that the infinite recursion that defines the comonad
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{G})
stops at the first step. We know this is the case for operads in the category of dg modules. We have to check that the lemmas proven for the category of dg modules also hold for the category of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules. This comes from the fact that the base field
\mathbb{K}
is of characteristic
0
, and hence for any
n
, the
\mathbb{K}
-algebra
\mathbb{K}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
is a semi-simple finite dimensional
\mathbb{K}
-algebra where every module is injective and projective. One can check that the different lemmas of
[
Ane14
, Section 3]
that make the proof work are also true in this context.
∎

Before defining the complete Bar construction relative to
\kappa
using the cofree dg-
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})
construction, one needs to understand coderivations on this object. We generalize the results of
[
GL18
, Section 6]
to our framework. Let
M
be a graded
\mathbb{S}
-module, we denote by
\pi_{M}
the map given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{M}:\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\eta_{u\mathcal{O}})(\mathrm{id})}

where
\eta_{u\mathcal{O}}:\I_{\mathbb{S}}\longrightarrow u\mathcal{O}
is the unit of the unital partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad
u\mathcal{O}
.

Lemma 6.23
.

Let
f:M\longrightarrow N
be a morphism graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules of degree
0
and
g:M\longrightarrow N
be a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules of degree
p
, then the degree
p
map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id}_{u\mathcal{O}})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(f,g)):\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(N)}

restricts to a degree
p
morphism
\mathscr{L}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(f,g)):\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)\longrightarrow\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(N)
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as
[
GL18
, Lemma 6.20]
. It is a consequence of the universal property of pullbacks: it suffices to construct a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules

\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(N)

that coincides with the morphism
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(f,g))
on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O}\circ u\mathcal{O})(N)
. One can check that this morphism is given by

\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})\left(\mbox{\tencyr SH}\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(f),\allowbreak\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(f,g))\right)\right)\circ p_{2}~.

∎

Proposition 6.24

(Coderivations on the cofree construction)
.

Let
M
be a graded
\mathbb{S}
-module and let
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)
be the cofree graded
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra generated by
M
. There is a natural bijection between maps

\varphi:\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)\longrightarrow M

of degree
p
and coderivations

d_{\varphi}:\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)\longrightarrow\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)

of degree
p
. This bijection sends
\varphi
to the degree
p
coderivation given by

d_{\varphi}\coloneqq\mathscr{L}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\pi_{M},\varphi))\cdot\omega(M)~,

where
\omega(M)
is the comonad structure map of
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

For any
\varphi
, the morphism
d_{\varphi}
is an endomorphism of
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(M)
by Lemma
6.23
. One has to check that it is a coderivation. The main point is to use the fact that
p_{1}
is a monomorphism to be able to check it directly on the dual Schur functors: the same diagrams as in
[
GL18
, Proposition 6.23]
commute, thus
d_{\varphi}
is a coderivation.
∎

We first present the formal constructions before giving a more explicit description of this adjunction.

Definition 6.25

(Complete Bar construction relative to
\kappa
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a complete curved
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-algebra. The
complete Bar construction relative to

\kappa
, denoted by
\widehat{\text{B}}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}
, is given by:

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}\coloneqq\left(\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P}),d_{\mathrm{bar}}=d_{1}+d_{2}\right)

where
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})
is the cofree dg
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra generated by the pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module
\mathcal{P}
. It is endowed with the pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{bar}}
given by the sum of two terms
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is of the pre-differential is the unique coderivation extending the following map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}:\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(d_{\mathcal{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{P}}}

It is given by

d_{1}=\mathscr{L}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathrm{id},d_{\mathcal{P}}))~.

The term
d_{2}
is given by the unique coderivation that extends the map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{2}:\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}}

Proposition 6.26
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a complete curved
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-algebra. The complete Bar construction
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}
forms a dg counital partial cooperad. Meaning that

d_{\mathrm{bar}}^{2}=0~.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of
[
GL18
, Proposition 9.2]
. In order to check that
d_{\mathrm{bar}}^{2}=0
, by a straightforward generalization of
[
GL18
, Proposition 6.25]
to the
\mathbb{S}
-colored case, it is enough to check that
(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot d_{\mathrm{bar}}=0
. We have that:

d_{\mathrm{bar}}=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\mathrm{id},d_{\mathcal{P}}))+\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id}_{u\mathcal{O}})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\pi_{\mathcal{P}},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}))(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}})\cdot p_{2}(\mathcal{P})~,

where the first term is
d_{1}
and the second is
d_{2}
. One computes that:

(\phi_{1}+\phi_{2})\cdot d_{\mathrm{bar}}=\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}\cdot\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa\star\kappa-\Theta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}}\cdot\eta_{u\mathcal{O}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}})=0~.

since
\kappa
is a curved twisting morphism. Therefore
d_{\mathrm{bar}}^{2}=0
and
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}
is a dg counital partial cooperad.
∎

Derivations on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(M)
, the free pdg
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-algebra on an pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module
M
, are completely characterized by their restrictions to the generators
M
. See
[
GL18
, Proposition 6.15]
for an analogous statement, which holds
mutatis mutandis
in our case. Let
M
be an pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module, we denote
\iota_{M}
be the following map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{M}:M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(M)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\epsilon)(id_{M})}

Definition 6.27

(Complete Cobar construction relative to
\kappa
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{C}\},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra. The
complete Cobar construction relative to

\kappa
, denoted by
\widehat{\Omega}_{\kappa}\mathcal{C}
, is given by:

\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C}),d_{\mathrm{cobar}}=d_{1}-d_{2}\right)

where
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})
is the free pdg
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-algebra. It is endowed with the pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}
which is the difference of two terms
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is the unique derivation that extends the map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}:\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(d_{\mathcal{C}})}

It is given by

d_{1}=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\mathrm{id},d_{\mathcal{C}}))~.

The term
d_{2}
is given by the unique derivation that extends the map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{2}:\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(u\mathcal{O})(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})}

Proposition 6.28
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{C}\},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra. The complete Cobar construction relative to
\kappa

\widehat{\Omega}_{\kappa}\mathcal{C}
forms a complete curved
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
-algebra. In other words, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\I_{\mathbb{S}})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})(\mathcal{C})~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Theta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-d_{\mathrm{cobar}}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Delta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})\cdot\varphi_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}}}

Proof.

The proof is again very similar to the proof of
[
GL18
, Proposition 9.1]
. In order to check that

-d_{\mathrm{cobar}}^{2}=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Delta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})\cdot\varphi_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}}\cdot\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Theta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})\circ\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id})~,

it is in fact enough to check that

-d_{\mathrm{cobar}}\cdot(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Theta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})~.

This comes from a straightforward generalization of
[
GL18
, Proposition 7.4]
. The pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}
is given by

d_{\mathrm{cobar}}=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\iota_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}}))-\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\Delta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\cdot\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\mbox{\tencyr SH}(\iota_{\mathcal{C}},\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})\cdot\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}))(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})~,

where the first term is
d_{1}
and the second is
d_{2}
. One computes that:

-d_{\mathrm{cobar}}\cdot(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})=\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathbb{S}}^{c}(\kappa\star\kappa)(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})~.

Since
\kappa
is a curved twisting morphism, this concludes the proof.
∎

Let us give explicit descriptions of these functors.

Definition 6.29

(Complete Cobar construction)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg counital partial cooperad. The
complete Cobar construction

\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
of
\mathcal{C}
is given by:

\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\left(\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu),d_{\mathrm{cobar}}=d_{1}-d_{2},\Theta_{\mathrm{cobar}}\right)~,

where
\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)
is the completed reduced tree monad applied to the dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu
. Here
\nu
is an arity
1
degree
-2
generator. It is endowed with the differential
d_{\mathrm{cobar}}
given by the difference of
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is the unique derivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}d_{\mathcal{C}}}

The term
d_{2}
comes from the
structure
of a dg counital partial cooperad on
\mathcal{C}
, it is given by the unique derivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\circ_{(1)}s^{-1}\mathcal{C})\oplus\I.\nu}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\I.\nu)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}\Delta_{(1)}+s^{-1}\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}}}

Its curvature
\Theta_{\mathrm{cobar}}
is given by the following map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathrm{cobar}}:\I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I.\nu}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\I.\nu)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}}

The resulting complete Cobar construction of
\mathcal{C}
forms a complete curved absolute partial operad.

Notation
.

We denote by
\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(-)
the cofree counital partial cooperad endofunctor in the category of pdg
\mathbb{S}
-modules. It is given by the cofree graded
u\mathcal{O}
-coalgebra
\mathscr{L}(u\mathcal{O})(-)
.

Definition 6.30

(Complete Bar construction)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})
a complete curved absolute partial operad. The
complete Bar construction

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}
of
\mathcal{P}
is given by:

\widehat{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\left(\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(s\mathcal{P}),d_{\mathrm{bar}}=d_{1}+d_{2}\right)~,

where
\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(s\mathcal{P})
is the cofree counital partial cooperad generated by the pdg
\mathbb{S}
-module
s\mathcal{P}
. It is endowed the pre-differential
d_{\mathrm{bar}}
, given by the sum of
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The term
d_{1}
is the unique coderivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(s\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sd_{\mathcal{P}}}

The term
d_{2}
comes from the
structure
of
\mathcal{P}
, it is given by the unique coderivation extending

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(s\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I\oplus(s\mathcal{P}\circ_{(1)}s\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}+s^{2}\gamma_{(1)}}

The resulting complete Bar construction of
\mathcal{P}
forms a dg counital partial cooperad.

Remark 6.31
.

The complete Bar construction of a complete curved absolute partial operad
\mathcal{P}
is, in general, not coaugmented. Indeed, one has that

d_{\mathrm{bar}}(|)=d_{2}(|)=\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathrm{id})~,

where
|
denotes the coaugmented counit of
\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(s\mathcal{P})
.

Proposition 6.32
.

The following holds:

(1)

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg counital partial cooperad. There is a natural isomorphism

\widehat{\Omega}_{\kappa}\mathcal{C}\cong\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}~.

(2)

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}})
a complete curved absolute partial operad. There is a natural isomorphism

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}\cong\widehat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P}~.

Proof.

This can be shown by direct inspection, extending the isomorphism of Lemma
9.34
.
∎

Lemma 6.33

(Curved convolution operad)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be a complete curved absolute partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg counital partial cooperad. The convolution pdg partial operad
(\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}),\{\circ_{i}\},\partial)
forms a curved partial operad endowed with the curvature given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}(\mathrm{id}):\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}}

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to Lemma
6.6
.
∎

Remark 6.34
.

We only used the operations
\{\circ_{i}\}
on
\mathcal{P}
in order to define this convolution operad. These operations are obtained by restricting the structural morphism
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
to finite sums inside the completed reduced tree monad. See Appendix B
Appendix A: What is an absolute partial operad ?
for more details. In fact, using the morphism
\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}
, one can show that this convolution curved partial operad is in fact an curved
absolute
partial operad. The idea is that infinite sums of convolution operations have a well-defined image in
\mathcal{P}
because
\mathcal{P}
is an absolute operad.

Moreover, the convolution operad between a conilpotent cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and an operad is
also
an
absolute operad
. Indeed, the decompositions in
\mathcal{C}
always produce finite sums, therefore infinite sums of convolution operations are still well-defined. For a more detailed discussion about how
convolution structures
always produce
absolute
types of algebraic structures, see
[
RiL22
, Section 4.1]
.

Once we have the curved convolution partial operad, we can define the notion of curved twisting morphism between complete curved absolute partial operads and dg counital partial cooperads.

Definition 6.35

(Curved twisting morphism)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})
be a complete curved absolute partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon,d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg counital partial cooperad. A
curved twisting morphism

\alpha
is a Maurer-Cartan element curved pre-Lie algebra given by the curved convolution operad:

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{C}(n),\mathcal{P}(n))~.

This is the data of a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
of a degree
-1
such that:

\partial(\alpha)+\alpha\star\alpha=\Theta_{\mathcal{H}om}(\mathrm{id})~.

The set of twisting morphism between
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
will be denoted
\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})
.

The set of curved twisting morphism between between complete curved partial operads and counital partial dg cooperads

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tw}(-,-):(\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop})^{\text{op}}\times\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}~,}

which is represented on both sides by the complete Bar-Cobar constructions.

Proposition 6.36

(Complete Bar-Cobar adjunction)
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a complete curved partial operad and let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon,d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg counital partial cooperad. There are isomorphisms:

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}}(\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}(\mathcal{C},\widehat{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{P})~,

which are natural in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

This can be shown by a straightforward computation.
∎

Corollary 6.37
.

There is a pair of adjoint functors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

7.
Counital partial cooperads up to homotopy and transfer of model structures

In this section, we introduce the notion of a counital partial cooperads up to homotopy. This notion is dual to that of unital partial operads up to homotopy developed in
[
Gri21
]
. In fact, there is a
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad which encodes unital partial operads up to homotopy as its algebras and counital partial cooperads up to homotopy as its coalgebras. The advantage of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy with respect to counital partial cooperads is that the admit a canonical cylinder object. Thus, using a left transfer theorem, we endow them with a model structure where weak equivalences are arity-wise quasi-isomorphisms. Notice that here, the category of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy with
strict morphisms
is the one being considered. Afterwards, we transfer this model structure to the category of complete curved absolute partial operads via a complete Bar-Cobar adjunction.

Notation
.

We denote by
\mathrm{RT}_{\omega}^{n}
the set of rooted trees of arity
n
with
\omega
internal edges. There is no restriction on the number of incoming edges of the vertices considered.

Definition 7.1

(Counital partial cooperad up to homotopy)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a dg
\mathbb{S}
-module. A
counital partial cooperad up to homotopy structure
on
\mathcal{C}
amounts to the data of a derivation of degree
-1

d:\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)

such that the restriction of
d
to
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}
is given by
s^{-1}d_{\mathcal{C}}
and such that for any series of rooted trees of arity
n
labeled by elements of
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}
:

d^{2}\left(\sum_{\omega\geq 1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathrm{RT}_{\omega}^{n}}\tau\right)=\sum_{\omega\geq 1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathrm{RT}_{\omega}^{n}}\left(v\circ_{1}\tau-\sum_{i=0}^{n}\tau\circ_{i}v\right)~.

In order words, the data of
\left(\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu),d\right)
forms a complete curved absolute partial operad.

Remark 7.2
.

The morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\I.\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

endows
\mathcal{C}
with a counit
\epsilon_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\I
which satisfies the counital axiom of counital partial cooperad only up to higher homotopies. The morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge~(2)}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

endows
\mathcal{C}
with a family of partial decompositions maps
\{\Delta_{i}\}
which satisfy the coparallel and cosequential axioms of a counital partial cooperad only up to higher homotopies. All the higher homotopies are given by the morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge~(\geq 3)}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

Example 7.3
.

Any dg counital partial cooperad
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},\epsilon,d_{\mathcal{C}})
is an example of counital partial cooperad up to homotopy via its complete Cobar construction
\widehat{\Omega}\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 7.4

(Strict morphisms)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},d_{1},d_{\mathcal{C}})
and
(\mathcal{D},d_{2},d_{\mathcal{D}})
be two counital partial cooperads up to homotopy. A
morphism

f:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
amounts to the data of a morphism of graded
\mathbb{S}
-modules
f:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
such that the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{D}\oplus\nu)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}f\oplus\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{2}}

Remark 7.5

(
\infty
-morphisms)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},d_{1},d_{\mathcal{C}})
and
(\mathcal{D},d_{2},d_{\mathcal{D}})
be two counital partial cooperads up to homotopy. An
\infty
-morphism

f:\mathcal{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{D}
amounts to the data of a morphism of complete curved absolute partial operads

f:\left(\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{C}\oplus\nu),d_{1}\right)\longrightarrow\left(\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}\mathcal{D}\oplus\nu),d_{2}\right)~.

These
\infty
-morphisms of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy should have all the expected properties of
\infty
-morphism between unital partial operads described in
[
Gri21
]
. In particular,
\infty
-quasi-isomorphisms of counital partial cooperads admit inverses in homology, and their set describes the hom-sets of the homotopy category. For the sake of brevity, we do not explore this path here.

Counital partial cooperads up to homotopy are coalgebras over a dg
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad, which is given by the Cobar construction on the conilpotent curved
\mathbb{S}
-colored partial cooperad
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
. This Cobar construction is the
\mathbb{S}
-colored analogous of the Cobar construction of Definition
6.14
. (We swear, dear reader, this is the last Cobar construction of the article !)

Definition 7.6

(The dg
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad encoding (co)unital partial (co)operads up to homotopy)
.

The dg
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad
\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})
is given by

\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)\coloneqq\left(\mathscr{T}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\right),d_{\mathrm{cobar}}=-d_{2}\right)~,

where
\mathscr{T}_{\mathbb{S}}(s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}))
is the free unital partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operad generated by the desuspension of the pdg
\mathbb{S}
-colored scheme
\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}
. It is endowed with a differential
d_{2}
, which is given by the unique derivation extending the map:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\circ_{(1)}s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\right)\oplus\I_{\mathbb{S}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(s^{-1}(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee})\right)~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{-2}\Delta_{(1)}\oplus s^{-1}\Theta_{\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}}}

Proposition 7.7
.

The following holds:

(1)

The category of unital partial operads up to homotopy with strict morphisms is equivalent to the category of dg algebras over
\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)
.

(2)

The category of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy with strict morphism is equivalent to the category of dg coalgebras over
\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)
.

Proof.

For the first statement, the proof is
mutatis mutandis
the same as the proof of
[
Gri21
, Proposition 36]
. For the second statement, the proof is
mutatis mutandis
the same as the proof of
[
GL18
, Theorem 12.1]
.
∎

Corollary 7.8
.

There forgetful functor from counital partial cooperads up to homotopy to the category of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules admits a right adjoint

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{upCoop}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathbb{S}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{L}\left(\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which is the cofree counital partial cooperad up to homotopy functor.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Theorem
6.22
.
∎

Using this adjunction, we want to transfer the standard model structure on dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules via a
left-transfer theorem
. The key ingredient to apply the left-transfer theorem in this situation is the construction of a
functorial cylinder object
in the category of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy.

Let
\mathrm{I}
be the commutative Hopf monoid given by the cellular chains on the interval
[0,1]
, see
[
BM03
, Example 3.3.3]
. It is a commutative algebra with a compatible coassociative coalgebra structure is given by the choice of a cellular diagonal on the topological interval. It is an interval object in the category of dg modules, meaning that there is a factorization

\mathbb{K}\oplus\mathbb{K}\rightarrowtail\mathrm{I}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathbb{K}

of the codiagonal map
\mathrm{id}\oplus\mathrm{id}:\mathbb{K}\oplus\mathbb{K}\longrightarrow\mathbb{K}
.

Definition 7.9

(Interval dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules)
.

The
interval
dg
\mathbb{S}
-module
\mathrm{Int}
is the dg
\mathbb{S}
-module given by

\mathrm{Int}(n)\coloneqq\mathrm{I}

for all
n\geq 0
.

Remark 7.10
.

If we endow
\mathrm{Int}
with the partial decomposition maps
\Delta_{i}:\mathrm{Int}(n+k-1)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Int}(n)\otimes\mathrm{Int}(k)
given by the coalgebra structure of
\mathrm{I}

\Delta:\mathrm{I}\longrightarrow\mathrm{I}\otimes\mathrm{I}~,

then
\mathrm{Int}

almost
forms a dg counital partial cooperad. It satisfies the cosequential axiom but, since
\Delta
is not
cocommutative
, it does not satisfy the coparallel axiom. If there existed a
cocommutative
interval object in the category of dg module, we could directly transfer the standard model structure of dg
\mathbb{S}
-module to the category of counital partial cooperads, in a dual version of
[
BM03
, Theorem 3.2]
. This is one reason to work with counital partial cooperads up to homotopy.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},d_{1},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a counital partial cooperad up to homotopy. The Hadamard product
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int}
has a canonical structure of counital partial cooperad up to homotopy.

Proof.

The proof is completely dual to the proof of
[
Gri21
, Proposition 31]
, where given a unital partial operad up to homotopy, the author builds a unital partial operad up to homotopy structure on
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathrm{Int}
.

Let us sketch this construction: the idea to build the map that induces the coderivation

\Delta:s^{-1}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int})\longrightarrow\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int})\oplus\nu)

is to do so by induction on the weight of rooted trees in
\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\wedge}
. Suppose there is a map

\Delta_{m-1}:s^{-1}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int})\longrightarrow\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\leq(m-1)}(s^{-1}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int})\oplus\nu)

such that

d^{2}_{m-1}\left(\sum_{\omega\geq 1}^{m-1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathrm{RT}_{\omega}^{n}}\tau\right)=\sum_{\omega\geq 1}^{m-1}\sum_{\tau\in\mathrm{RT}_{\omega}^{n}}\left(v\circ_{1}\tau-\sum_{i=0}^{n}\tau\circ_{i}v\right)~,

where
d_{m-1}
is the induced coderivation of
\overline{\mathscr{T}}^{\leq(m-1)}
by
\Delta_{m-1}
. Furthermore, we suppose that the following equalities hold

\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}~(s\pi\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}})=(s\pi\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{\nu})^{\otimes(m-1)}~\Delta_{m-1}~,

(s\iota_{i}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}}\oplus\mathrm{Id}_{\nu})^{\otimes(m-1)}~\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}=\Delta_{m-1}~(s\iota_{i}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{C}})~,

where
p
is the projection
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
and
\iota_{i}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int}
, for
i=1,2
are the two inclusions. Notice that these two conditions imply the functoriality of this construction. Extending
\Delta_{m-1}
to
\Delta_{m}
can be shown to be equivalent to finding a lift in a commutative square
in the underlying category of dg modules. This lift because in the square, the left hand side is a cofibration and the right hand side is an acyclic fibration.
∎

Corollary 7.12

(Functorial cylinder object)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},d_{1},d_{\mathcal{C}})
be a counital partial cooperad up to homotopy. Then the codiagonal morphism
\mathrm{id}\oplus\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{C}\oplus\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
factors as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\oplus\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the first arrow is a degree-wise monomorphism and the second arrow is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism. Thus
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathrm{Int}
is a cylinder object which is functorial in
\mathcal{C}
.

Theorem 7.13

(Transferred model structure)
.

There is a cofibrantly generated model structure on the category of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy defined by the following classes of morphisms:

(1)

The class of weak-equivalences
\mathrm{W}
is given by strict morphisms of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy
f:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
such that
f(n):\mathcal{C}(n)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(n)
is a quasi-isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.

(2)

The class of cofibrations is
\mathrm{Cof}
is given by strict morphisms of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy
f:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
such that
f(n):\mathcal{C}(n)\rightarrowtail\mathcal{D}(n)
is a degree-wise monomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.

(3)

The class of fibrations
\mathrm{Fib}
is given by strict morphisms of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy which have the right lifting property against all morphism in
\mathrm{W}\cap\mathrm{Cof}
.

Proof.

In order to apply the left-transfer theorem such as
[
HKRS17
]
, essentially need a functorial cofibrant replacement functor and a functorial cylinder object. Since every object is cofibrant in this model structure, we only need a functorial cylinder object. This follows from Corollary
7.12
.
∎

Now our aim is to transfer this structure to category of complete curved absolute partial operads, replicating the methods of Section 10 in
[
GL18
]
. For this purpose, we induce a complete Bar-Cobar adjunction using a curved twisting morphism between the
\mathbb{S}
-colored objects encoding these categories. (Yes, dear reader, we lied). For that, we consider the universal curved twisting morphism
\iota
:

Lemma 7.14
.

The
\mathbb{S}
-color scheme map given by the inclusion

\iota:\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)

is a curved twisting morphism.

Proof.

This is immediate to check from the definition.
∎

Proposition 7.15

(Complete Bar-Cobar adjunction relative to
\iota
)
.

There is a pair of adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{upCoop}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

The constructions of the complete Bar and the complete Cobar functors are
mutatis mutandis
the same as those described in the previous section, where we constructed the complete Bar-Cobar relative to
\kappa
. The proof that these functors form an adjunction is also
mutatis mutandis
the same.
∎

This allows us in turn to transfer the above model structure from counital partial cooperads up to homotopy to complete curve absolute partial operads using a
right transfer theorem
.

Theorem 7.16
.

There is a cofibrantly generated model structure on the category of complete curved absolute partial operads defined by the following classes of morphisms:

(1)

The class of weak-equivalences
\mathrm{W}
is given by morphisms of complete curved absolute partial operads
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(f)(n):\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathcal{P})(n)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathcal{Q})(n)
is a quasi-isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.

(2)

The class of fibrations is
\mathrm{Fib}
is given by morphisms of complete curved absolute partial operads
f:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
such that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(f):\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathcal{Q})
is a fibration of counital partial cooperads up to homotopy.

(3)

The class of cofibrations
\mathrm{Cof}
is given by morphisms of complete curved absolute partial operads which have the left lifting property against all morphism in
\mathrm{W}\cap\mathrm{Fib}
.

Proof.

In order to apply the right transfer theorem, we need to check that the acyclicity conditions of
[
BM03
, Section 2.5]
are satisfied. Thus these conditions need to be checked by direct computation, as it is done in
[
GL18
, Section 10.5]
. The key point is to show that acyclic cofibrations of complete curved partial cooperads are precisely given by graded quasi-isomorphisms of Definition
7.18
. Then the result follows, as it is clear that the complete Cobar functor preserve small objects, and since these trivial cofibrations are stable under pushouts and sequential colimits. In is a straightforward but tedious exercise to check that the arguments used in
loc.cit
generalize to our framework.
∎

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
f:(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{Q},\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}},d_{\mathcal{Q}},\Theta_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a morphism of complete curved absolute partial operads. If
f(n):\mathcal{P}(n)\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}(n)
is a degree-wise epimorphism, then it is a fibration. Thus all complete curved absolute partial operads are fibrant.

Proof.

The arguments of Section 10.2 in
[
GL18
]
generalize to the operadic setting
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

Definition 7.18

(Graded quasi-isomorphism)
.

Let
f:(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{Q},\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}},d_{\mathcal{Q}},\Theta_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a morphism of complete curved absolute partial operads. It is a
graded quasi-isomorphism
if the induced morphism of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules

\mathrm{gr}(f):\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{P})\cong\bigoplus_{\omega\geq 1}\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}\mathcal{P}/\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega+1}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{Q})\cong\bigoplus_{\omega\geq 1}\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}\mathcal{Q}/\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega+1}\mathcal{Q}

is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism. Here
\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\omega}\mathcal{P}
denotes the
\omega
-term of the canonical filtration of an absolute partial operad defined in Appendix
Appendix A: What is an absolute partial operad ?
.

Remark 7.19
.

One checks easily that since
d_{\mathcal{P}}^{2}
raises the weight of an operation in
\mathcal{P}
by one, it is equal to zero in the associated graded. Therefore
\mathrm{gr}(\mathcal{P})
forms a dg
\mathbb{S}
-module.

Proposition 7.20
.

Let
f:(\mathcal{P},\gamma_{\mathcal{P}},d_{\mathcal{P}},\Theta_{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{Q},\gamma_{\mathcal{Q}},d_{\mathcal{Q}},\Theta_{\mathcal{Q}})
be a graded quasi-isomorphism between two complete curved absolute partial operads. Then it is a weak-equivalence, meaning that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(f):\widehat{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(\mathcal{Q})
is an arity-wise quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is completely analogous to that of
[
GL18
, Theorem 10.23]
. It is essentially done by induction. The cofree counital cooperad functor preserves quasi-isomorphisms. Thus it sends graded quasi-isomorphisms between curved partial operads endowed with the trivial operad structure to quasi-isomorphisms. Then one shows a weak version of the five-lemma
[
GL18
, Lemma 10.20]
which allows us to go from weight
\omega
to weight
\omega+1
. See Section 10.3 for more details on that matter.
∎

Remark 7.21
.

The above proposition implies that the weak-equivalences of complete curved operads as defined in
[
DCBM20
]
by graded quasi-isomorphisms should be strictly included inside the weak-equivalences of our model structure. Nevertheless, the objects and the underlying categories considered are quite different in nature, so we do not attempt a precise comparison result.

There is a morphism of dg unital partial
\mathbb{S}
-colored operads
f_{\kappa}:\Omega_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{S}\otimes c\mathcal{O}^{\vee}\right)\longrightarrow u\mathcal{O}
, which in turn induces a morphism between the associated comonads in the category of dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules. Thus one has an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{upCoop}_{\infty}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}_{f_{\kappa}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coind}_{f_{\kappa}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Res}_{f_{\kappa}}
is fully faithful and preserves quasi-isomorphisms.

Proposition 7.22
.

There following triangle of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 12.0pt\mathsf{upCoop}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 30.0pt\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coind}_{f_{\kappa}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}_{f_{\kappa}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\text{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\text{B}}_{\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

commutes up to natural isomorphism.

Proof.

It is immediate to check that the left-adjoints are naturally isomorphic. Thus by mates of adjunction, the right adjoints are also naturally isomorphic.
∎

Remark 7.23
.

We believe that the complete Bar-Cobar adjunction relative to
\iota
is a Quillen equivalence. One could generalize the arguments in Section 11 of
[
GL18
]
to the
\mathbb{S}
-colored framework in order to prove this.

On the other hand, one could try to induce a model structure on counital partial cooperads using the forgetful-cofree adjunction and transfer it to the category of complete curved absolute partial operads. It is not clear to us that these model structures should coincide. Indeed, this would amount to proving that the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{upCoop}_{\infty}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}_{f_{\kappa}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coind}_{f_{\kappa}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen equivalence. But this adjunction restricts to the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~u\mathcal{A}ss\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathsf{coalg}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}_{f_{\kappa}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Coind}_{f_{\kappa}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

when one restricts to dg
\mathbb{S}
-modules concentrated in arity one. It is not clear at all that this adjunction is a Quillen equivalence, see
[
GL18
, Conjecture 8.10]
.

8.
Duality functors and Koszul duality

In this section, we build duality functors that intertwine the two Bar-Cobar adjunctions defined in Section 6, forming an algebraic duality square of commuting adjunctions. On the homotopical side of things, we build a second duality square of commuting Quillen adjunctions when (co)unital partial (co)operads are replaced by their "up to homotopy" counterparts. Using these squares, we compute minimal cofibrant resolutions for complete curved absolute partial operads in certain cases of interest.

8.1.
Algebraic duality square square

Our goal is to construct left adjoint functors to the linear dual functor that sends "coalgebraic objects" into "algebraic objects".

Lemma 8.1
.

The linear duality functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}

admits a left adjoint.

Proof.

Consider the following square of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathbb{S}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathbb{S}\text{-}\mathsf{mod}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(-)\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

First notice that the adjunction on the left hand side is monadic, since we consider the opposite of a comonadic adjunction. Furthermore, all categories involved are complete and cocomplete. It is absolutely clear that
(-)^{*}\cdot\mathrm{U}^{\mathsf{op}}\cong\mathrm{U}\cdot(-)^{*}~.
Thus we can apply the Adjoint Lifting Theorem
[
Joh75
, Theorem 2]
to this situation, which concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 8.2

(Sweedler dual)
.

The
Sweedler duality functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}

is defined as the left adjoint of the linear dual functor.

Remark 8.3
.

The proof of the Adjoint Lifting Theorem
[
Joh75
, Theorem 2]
gives an explicit construction of this left adjoint. Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad, and let

\gamma_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}

be its structural morphism as an algebra over the tree monad. The Sweelder dual dg counital partial cooperad
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}
is given by the following equalizer:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Eq}\Bigg(\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(\mathcal{P}^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}^{\vee}\left((\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{P}))^{*}\right)\Bigg)~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\gamma_{\mathcal{P}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varrho}

where
\varrho
is an arrow constructed using the comonadic structure of
\mathscr{T}^{\vee}
and the canonical inclusio of a dg
\mathbb{S}
-module into its double linear dual.

Remark 8.4
.

If we restrict to unital partial operads concentrated in arity
1
, that is, unital associative algebras, the Sweedler dual functor we have constructed is naturally isomorphic with the original Sweedler dual of
[
Swe69
]
.

Remark 8.5
.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\{\circ_{i}\},\eta,d_{\mathcal{P}})
be a dg unital partial operad such that
\mathcal{P}(n)
is degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above or below. Its Sweedler dual
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}
is simply given by
(\mathcal{P}^{*},\{\circ_{i}^{*}\},\eta^{*},d_{\mathcal{P}}^{*})
. The adjunction constructed restricts to an anti-equivalence of categories between dg counital partial cooperads which are arity-wise degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above or below and dg unital partial operads which are arity-wise degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded above or below.

Let’s turn to the other side of the Koszul duality. We postpone the following proofs and constructions to the Appendix
Appendix A: What is an absolute partial operad ?
, where there is a detailed discussion of absolute partial operads and their properties.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\{\Delta_{i}\},d_{\mathcal{C}},\Theta_{\mathcal{C}})
be a conilpotent curved partial cooperad. Then its linear dual
\mathcal{C}^{*}
has inherits a structure of a complete curved absolute partial operad. This defines a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}

Proof.

See Lemma
9.37
.
∎

Proposition 8.7
.

The linear duality functor admits a left adjoint. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

See Proposition
9.38
.
∎

Definition 8.8

(Topological dual functor)
.

The
topological dual functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

is defined as the left adjoint of the linear dual functor.

This allows us to construct the first duality square of commuting functors.

Theorem 8.9

(Duality square)
.

The following square of adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upOp}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{pCoop}^{\mathsf{conil}}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}~\mathsf{upCoop}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{curv}~\mathsf{abs}~\mathsf{pOp}^{\mathsf{comp}}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\mathsf{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

commutes in the following sense: right adjoints going from the top right to the bottom left are naturally isomorphic.

Proof.

Let us show the commutativity of this square. We have, for any graded
\mathbb{S}
-module
M
, a natural isomorphism of graded counital partial cooperads

\mathscr{T}^{\vee}(sM^{*})\cong\left(\mathscr{T}(s^{-1}M)\right)^{\circ}~.
